user
Design v170 from v160 using a materially different elementwise CTA-grid architecture while v169 awaits C500 access recovery. Do not inspect xpuoj/ or old fused_moe_gemm/.
Current pack, guarded SwiGLU, and unpack kernels use grid(expert,Nblock) and `T.Parallel(expert_block_m=192, block_n=256)` inside one CTA, so each CTA covers all192 rows. Proposal: coordinated M-grid split with block_m=64: grid(expert,3,Nblock), `T.Parallel(64,256)`, direct row expression `bm*64+i`; apply to pack, guarded SwiGLU, and each unpack kernel. Tensor math/layout/BMMs/chunks unchanged. This is not prior block_n widening or GEMM chunk tuning. It increases CTA count3x but reduces work per CTA3x and may improve occupancy/latency across elementwise stages.
Rank all-shape versus E64-only versus E16-only M-grid split. Audit history for duplicate M-axis elementwise scheduling. Avoid local scalar assignments inside T.Parallel that triggered static race warnings; use direct index expressions/guards. Estimate stage-level upside and launch-grid costs. Select one coherent prototype.
Output DUPLICATE_AUDIT, SHAPE_POLICY, SELECTED_ARCHITECTURE, KERNEL_GRID_CHANGES, INDEXING_PROOF, RACE_PROOF, WORK_AND_OCCUPANCY_MODEL, EXPECTED_GAIN, RISKS, IMPLEMENTATION_INSTRUCTIONS, FINAL_GATE PASS_TO_IMPLEMENT or BLOCKED.

import torch
import tilelang
import tilelang.language as T


_KERNEL_CACHE = {}
_WORKSPACE_CACHE_KEY = None
_WORKSPACE_CACHE_VALUE = None
_WEIGHT_CACHE = {}
_EXPERT_BLOCK_M = 192


@tilelang.jit(
    pass_configs={
        tilelang.PassConfigKey.TL_DISABLE_WARP_SPECIALIZED: True,
        tilelang.PassConfigKey.TL_DISABLE_SAFE_MEMORY_ACCESS: True,
    }
)
def _pack_experts_kernel(
    hidden,
    num_experts,
    total_padded_tokens,
    expert_block_m,
):
    block_n = 256
    threads = 256
    packed_shape = (num_experts, expert_block_m, hidden)

    @T.prim_func
    def kernel(
        stacked_expert_tokens: T.Tensor((total_padded_tokens, hidden), T.float16),
        group_sizes: T.Tensor((num_experts,), T.int32),
        group_padded_offsets: T.Tensor((num_experts + 1,), T.int32),
        packed_input: T.Tensor(packed_shape, T.float16),
    ):
        with T.Kernel(
            num_experts,
            T.ceildiv(hidden, block_n),
            threads=threads,
        ) as (expert_id, bn):
            group_size = group_sizes[expert_id]
            padded_start = group_padded_offsets[expert_id]
            for i, j in T.Parallel(expert_block_m, block_n):
                if i < group_size:
                    packed_input[expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                        stacked_expert_tokens[padded_start + i, bn * block_n + j]
                    )

    return kernel


@tilelang.jit(
    pass_configs={
        tilelang.PassConfigKey.TL_DISABLE_WARP_SPECIALIZED: True,
        tilelang.PassConfigKey.TL_DISABLE_SAFE_MEMORY_ACCESS: True,
    }
)
def _unpack_experts_kernel(
    hidden,
    num_experts,
    total_padded_tokens,
    total_valid_tokens,
    expert_block_m,
    expert_start,
    expert_chunk,
):
    block_n = 256
    threads = 256
    packed_shape = (expert_chunk, expert_block_m, hidden)

    @T.prim_func
    def kernel(
        packed_output: T.Tensor(packed_shape, T.float16),
        routed_expert_weights: T.Tensor((total_valid_tokens,), T.float32),
        group_sizes: T.Tensor((num_experts,), T.int32),
        group_offsets: T.Tensor((num_experts + 1,), T.int32),
        group_padded_offsets: T.Tensor((num_experts + 1,), T.int32),
        out: T.Tensor((total_padded_tokens, hidden), T.float16),
    ):
        with T.Kernel(
            expert_chunk,
            T.ceildiv(hidden, block_n),
            threads=threads,
        ) as (local_expert_id, bn):
            expert_id = expert_start + local_expert_id
            group_size = group_sizes[expert_id]
            raw_start = group_offsets[expert_id]
            padded_start = group_padded_offsets[expert_id]
            for i, j in T.Parallel(expert_block_m, block_n):
                if i < group_size:
                    out[padded_start + i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                        packed_output[local_expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j]
                        * routed_expert_weights[raw_start + i]
                    )

    return kernel

def _swiglu_kernel(
    intermediate,
    num_experts,
    expert_block_m,
):
    block_n = 256
    threads = 256
    shape = (num_experts, expert_block_m, intermediate)

    @T.prim_func
    def kernel(
        gate_output: T.Tensor(shape, T.float16),
        up_output: T.Tensor(shape, T.float16),
        group_sizes: T.Tensor((num_experts,), T.int32),
        activation: T.Tensor(shape, T.float16),
    ):
        with T.Kernel(
            num_experts,
            T.ceildiv(intermediate, block_n),
            threads=threads,
        ) as (expert_id, bn):
            group_size = group_sizes[expert_id]
            for i, j in T.Parallel(expert_block_m, block_n):
                if i < group_size:
                    gate = gate_output[expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j]
                    activation[expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                        gate
                        * T.sigmoid(gate)
                        * up_output[expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j]
                    )

    return kernel

    }

def _combine_fc1_weights_kernel(
    hidden,
    intermediate,
    num_experts,
):
    block_i = 64
    block_h = 256
    threads = 256

    @T.prim_func
    def kernel(
        gate_w: T.Tensor((num_experts, intermediate, hidden), T.float16),
        up_w: T.Tensor((num_experts, intermediate, hidden), T.float16),
        combined_w: T.Tensor((num_experts, intermediate * 2, hidden), T.float16),
    ):
        with T.Kernel(
            num_experts,
            T.ceildiv(intermediate * 2, block_i),
            T.ceildiv(hidden, block_h),
            threads=threads,
        ) as (expert_id, bi, bh):
            for i, j in T.Parallel(block_i, block_h):
                row = bi * block_i + i
                col = bh * block_h + j
                if row < intermediate:
                    combined_w[expert_id, row, col] = gate_w[expert_id, row, col]
                else:
                    combined_w[expert_id, row, col] = up_w[
                        expert_id, row - intermediate, col
                    ]

    return kernel


@tilelang.jit(
    pass_configs={
        tilelang.PassConfigKey.TL_DISABLE_WARP_SPECIALIZED: True,
        tilelang.PassConfigKey.TL_DISABLE_SAFE_MEMORY_ACCESS: True,
    }
)
def _combined_swiglu_kernel(
    intermediate,
    num_experts,
    expert_block_m,
):
    block_n = 256
    threads = 256
    combined_shape = (num_experts, expert_block_m, intermediate * 2)
    activation_shape = (num_experts, expert_block_m, intermediate)

    @T.prim_func
    def kernel(
        gate_up_output: T.Tensor(combined_shape, T.float16),
        activation: T.Tensor(activation_shape, T.float16),
    ):
        with T.Kernel(
            num_experts,
            T.ceildiv(intermediate, block_n),
            threads=threads,
        ) as (expert_id, bn):
            for i, j in T.Parallel(expert_block_m, block_n):
                gate = gate_up_output[expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j]
                activation[expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                    gate
                    * T.sigmoid(gate)
                    * gate_up_output[
                        expert_id,
                        i,
                        intermediate + bn * block_n + j,
                    ]
                )

    return kernel

def _get_workspace(
    stacked_expert_tokens,
    intermediate,
    num_experts,
    expert_block_m,
):
    global _WORKSPACE_CACHE_KEY, _WORKSPACE_CACHE_VALUE
    hidden = int(stacked_expert_tokens.shape[1])
    key = (
        int(stacked_expert_tokens.device.index or 0),
        int(num_experts),
        hidden,
        int(intermediate),
        int(expert_block_m),
        str(stacked_expert_tokens.dtype),
    )
    if _WORKSPACE_CACHE_KEY != key:
        _WORKSPACE_CACHE_KEY = None
        _WORKSPACE_CACHE_VALUE = None
        packed_input = torch.empty(
            (num_experts, expert_block_m, hidden),
            device=stacked_expert_tokens.device,
            dtype=stacked_expert_tokens.dtype,
        )
        activation = (
            torch.empty(
                (num_experts, expert_block_m, intermediate),
                device=stacked_expert_tokens.device,
                dtype=stacked_expert_tokens.dtype,
            )
            if False
            else None
        )
        _WORKSPACE_CACHE_KEY = key
        _WORKSPACE_CACHE_VALUE = (packed_input, activation)
    return _WORKSPACE_CACHE_VALUE


def _get_combined_fc1_weight(gate_w, up_w, combine_weights):
    key = (
        int(gate_w.device.index or 0),
        int(gate_w.shape[0]),
        int(gate_w.shape[1]),
        int(gate_w.shape[2]),
        str(gate_w.dtype),
    )
    cached = _WEIGHT_CACHE.get(key)
    if cached is None or cached[0] is not gate_w or cached[1] is not up_w:
        combined_w = torch.empty(
            (gate_w.shape[0], gate_w.shape[1] * 2, gate_w.shape[2]),
            device=gate_w.device,
            dtype=gate_w.dtype,
        )
        combine_weights(gate_w, up_w, combined_w)
        cached = (gate_w, up_w, combined_w)
        _WEIGHT_CACHE[key] = cached
    return cached[2]


def run_kernel(
    stacked_expert_tokens,
    gate_w,
    up_w,
    down_w,
    routed_expert_weights,
    group_sizes,
    group_offsets,
    group_padded_offsets,
    group_idx_for_bx,
    out,
):
    hidden = int(stacked_expert_tokens.shape[1])
    intermediate = int(gate_w.shape[1])
    num_experts = int(gate_w.shape[0])
    total_padded_tokens = int(stacked_expert_tokens.shape[0])
    total_valid_tokens = int(routed_expert_weights.shape[0])

    expert_block_m = _EXPERT_BLOCK_M

    pack, swiglu, combine_weights, combined_swiglu, unpack_chunks = _get_kernels(
        hidden,
        intermediate,
        num_experts,
        total_padded_tokens,
        total_valid_tokens,
        expert_block_m,
    )
    packed_input, activation = _get_workspace(
        stacked_expert_tokens,
        intermediate,
        num_experts,
        expert_block_m,
    )

    pack(
        stacked_expert_tokens,
        group_sizes,
        group_padded_offsets,
        packed_input,
    )
    if num_experts == 16:
        up_output = packed_input @ up_w.transpose(1, 2)
        gate_output = packed_input @ gate_w.transpose(1, 2)
        swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, up_output)
        del gate_output
        activation = up_output
    else:
        gate_output = packed_input @ gate_w.transpose(1, 2)
        up_output = packed_input @ up_w.transpose(1, 2)
        swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, gate_output)
        del up_output
        activation = gate_output
    expert_chunk = 32 if num_experts == 32 else 16
    for chunk_id, expert_start in enumerate(range(0, num_experts, expert_chunk)):
        expert_end = min(expert_start + expert_chunk, num_experts)
        packed_output = (
            activation[expert_start:expert_end]
            @ down_w[expert_start:expert_end].transpose(1, 2)
        )
        unpack_chunks[chunk_id](
            packed_output,
            routed_expert_weights,
            group_sizes,
            group_offsets,
            group_padded_offsets,
            out,
        )
        del packed_output

def _swiglu_kernel(
    intermediate,
    num_experts,
    expert_block_m,
):
    block_n = 256
    threads = 256
    shape = (num_experts, expert_block_m, intermediate)

    @T.prim_func
    def kernel(
        gate_output: T.Tensor(shape, T.float16),
        up_output: T.Tensor(shape, T.float16),
        group_sizes: T.Tensor((num_experts,), T.int32),
        activation: T.Tensor(shape, T.float16),
    ):
        with T.Kernel(
            num_experts,
            T.ceildiv(intermediate, block_n),
            threads=threads,
        ) as (expert_id, bn):
            group_size = group_sizes[expert_id]
            for i, j in T.Parallel(expert_block_m, block_n):
                if i < group_size:
                    gate = gate_output[expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j]
                    if num_experts == 16:
                        activation[expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                            gate
                            / (T.float32(1) + T.exp(-gate))
                            * up_output[expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j]
                        )
                    else:
                        activation[expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                            gate
                            * T.sigmoid(gate)
                            * up_output[expert_id, i, bn * block_n + j]
                        )

    return kernel

- Codex implementation gate: all three points and path-isolation checks passed.

- Timings: 2.099 (sample 2.100) / 3.725 / 6.660 ms.
- Comparison with v112: approximately +2.99% / +0.65% / +1.19%; slower in every case.
- Conclusion: larger E16 elementwise blocks are correct but increase runtime; do not retry block512 immediately. Return E16 to block256 and investigate stage-level memory traffic or temporary pressure instead.

### v133_single_entry_workspace_cache.py

- Parent: v112.
- Hypothesis: eliminate cross-shape workspace retention that contributed to resource instability by replacing the multi-key workspace dictionary with one exception-safe strong-reference key/value slot; kernels and tensor math remain unchanged.
- Codex implementation loop caught an exception-safety issue: clearing only the old value could leave a stale key if allocation failed. Final ordering clears key, clears value, allocates, then publishes the new key/value.
- C500: all three official shapes passed, with max errors 0.0009765625 / 0.00390625 / 0.00390625, zero padding, and immutable inputs.
- XPUOJ submission 105059: Accepted, score 83.67, at 2.042 / 3.701 / 6.630 ms (sample case1 2.044).
- Versus original v112: +0.004 / 0.000 / +0.048 ms. Versus fresh v112 control 105048: -0.018 / -0.025 / +0.004 ms. Codex classifies this as timing-neutral within OJ noise.
- Baseline decision: adopt v133 as the operational parent because it retains only one shape workspace and reduces multi-shape allocator pressure without correctness or score loss. Keep original v112 as the historical best timing reference.

### v134_local_down_weight_transpose.py

Adversarial review rejected it because a view would keep the full source weights alive across testcases and could worsen generator OOM. ### v135_skip_pack_padding_zero.py

- Parent: v133.
- Hypothesis: remove pack-kernel zero stores for `i >= group_size`; those padding rows are processed independently by FC1/SwiGLU/FC2 and never read by unpack, so stale values cannot affect real outputs.
- Codex design ranking selected this over E16 compaction and custom fused unpack due lower correctness and compile risk. Static verification proved real-row writes, row independence, unpack read bounds, and workspace reuse safety.
- C500: all three official shapes passed, with max errors 0.0009765625 / 0.00390625 / 0.00390625, zero final output padding, and immutable inputs.

### v161 E16 layout investigation (blocked before implementation)

- Parent would have been v160.
- Investigated zero-copy E16 input views, 128-core/64-overflow splitting, and E16 routed-weight prescaling.
- Layout proof blocked zero-copy: physical input uses irregular per-expert 128-row padded segments (`3840` physical rows versus `16*192=3072` dense rows), so no regular `[16,192,H]` view exists.
- Static two-source splitting saves no GEMM rows and adds launches; dynamic overflow compaction has no OJ-safe construction path with currently allowed APIs. E16 prescaling has an unfavorable intermediate/hidden ratio.
- No v161 source, C500 run, or XPUOJ submission.

### v164 output-initialization reuse (blocked before implementation)

- Proposed replacing full-output zero initialization with empty allocation plus selective padding clear.
- Gate review found the submission does not own output allocation: `out` is supplied by the official runner, so changing `zeros_like` is outside the solution boundary. Reusing `out` as packed workspace also lacks a hard storage-capacity contract.
- No v164 source, C500 run, or XPUOJ submission.

### v163_e64_hybrid_fc2_tail.py

- Parent: v160.
- Hybrid E64 experiment: retain high-level chunk16 FC2 BMM on rows0:128 and compute only rows128:group_size with a guarded custom TileLang tail FC2, with disjoint head/tail unpack.
- Codex implementation and C500 numerical gates passed all official shapes.
- XPUOJ submission 107204: Accepted, score 79.33, at 2.024 / 3.674 / 14.205 ms.
- Changed E64 regressed +7.648 ms (+116.6%, 2.17x); aggregate regressed62.5%.
- Conclusion: retire both hybrid and full custom FC2 families. The backend BMM advantage dominates even when the custom kernel computes only tail rows; no tile/stage/threshold tuning is justified.

### v165_all_shapes_fc1_sequential_free.py

- Parent: v160.
- Sole cross-shape completion of v160's E16 FC1 order/lifetime idea: E32/E64 also compute up before gate, preserve up storage as activation, and release gate.
- Codex implementation and C500 numerical gates passed all official shapes.
- XPUOJ submission 107228: Accepted, score 83.67, at 2.049 / 3.714 / 6.620 ms.
- Versus v160: +1.54% / +1.12% / +0.96%; every case and aggregate regressed.
- Conclusion: rollback and close the all-shape FC1 order/in-place family. Retain the E16-only v160 specialization.

### v166_e32_storage_only.py

- Parent: v160.
- Factorial ablation: E32 retained the original gate-then-up FC1 order but changed only the surviving SwiGLU storage to `up_output`; E16/E64 retained v160.
- Codex implementation and C500 numerical gates passed all shapes.
- XPUOJ submission 107242: Accepted, score 83.67, at 2.042 / 3.719 / 6.598 ms.
- Changed E32 regressed +0.046 ms (+1.25%). Together with v165 E32 at3.714 ms, both storage-only and storage+order are negative.
- Conclusion: rollback and close the E32 FC1 storage/order factorial family.

codex
I’m also checking the recorded timing envelope around v149-v169 so the recommendation accounts for judge noise and shape-specific regressions, not just static code plausibility.

### v167_e16_order_only.py

- Parent: v160.
- Final E16 factorial ablation: retain up-before-gate order but restore gate storage as SwiGLU output, isolating order from storage reuse.
- Codex and C500 numerical gates passed.
- XPUOJ submissions 107262 and unchanged retry 107269 both produced case1 WrongAnswer, with large element errors 0.1533 and 0.1265 respectively.
- Conclusion: E16 up-before-gate is not OJ-safe when gate storage survives. The accepted v160 behavior depends on the coupled up-storage destination; order-only is permanently blocked.

### v168_e64_exp_swiglu.py

- Parent: v160.
- E64-only numerical primitive experiment: replace native `T.sigmoid` with exact natural-exp form `gate / (1 + exp(-gate))`; E16/E32 unchanged.
- Codex and C500 gates passed. C500 E64 mean error improved from about2.94e-4 to2.31e-4, while max error stayed0.00390625.
- XPUOJ submission 107290: Accepted, score83.67, at2.051 /3.708 /6.649 ms.
- Changed E64 regressed +0.092 ms (+1.40%); aggregate regressed1.31%.
- Conclusion: rollback E64 explicit-exp. Historical routed evidence leaves exactly one E16-only explicit-exp closure test before retiring this primitive family.

## Current Baseline

- Operational parent: `v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py`, XPUOJ submission 107154, Accepted score 83.67, timings 2.018 / 3.673 / 6.557 ms.
- Historical performance references: v149 submission 106766 at 2.027 / 3.670 / 6.567 and v141 submission 105768 at 2.024 / 3.695 / 6.563.
- Measured performance envelope from v136/v141: approximately 2.02-2.03 / 3.68-3.70 / 6.56-6.57 ms.
- Historical best timing reference: `v112_e32_chunk32_fused_e64_chunk16.py`, XPUOJ submission 104790, score 83.67, timings 2.038 / 3.701 / 6.582 ms.
- Fresh v112 control submission 105048: Accepted score 83.67 at 2.060 / 3.726 / 6.626 ms; use this as a same-session noise reference.
- Timings: 2.038 / 3.701 / 6.582 ms for cases 1/2/3.
- Architecture: packed per-expert dense input with M=192; ordinary two-BMM FC1 for all cases; FC2 chunks 16/32/16 for E=16/E=32/E=64; separate SwiGLU; weighted unpack; Python kernel/workspace caching.
- `v100_packed_dense_81_baseline.py` remains the original score-81 reference, not the active baseline.
- Do not inspect or use repository `xpuoj/` or old `fused_moe_gemm/` experiments. A version counts only after a fresh current-XPUOJ Accepted result.
- C500 remains compile/numerical validation only; performance decisions come from current XPUOJ.

codex
**DUPLICATE_AUDIT**

No duplicate found for the proposed pack/SwiGLU/unpack M-grid split. Prior related work was different:

- v131/v139: elementwise `block_n=512`, width-only, both regressed.
- v123-v125: `block_n=128/512`-style elementwise width probes, not an M-axis grid split.
- v140: `block_m=64` inside a custom fused FC1/SwiGLU TileLang GEMM path, not current pack/SwiGLU/unpack kernels.
- v163: M split inside custom FC2 tail, retired because backend BMM dominated.

So this is a materially new elementwise CTA-grid architecture.

**SHAPE_POLICY**

Ranking:

1. `E16-only`: best risk/reward. E16 has the lowest existing elementwise CTA count and v160’s only positive signal.
2. `all-shape`: broader experiment, but likely lets E32/E64 scheduling overhead erase E16 gains.
3. `E64-only`: weakest. E64 already has enough elementwise CTAs; 3x CTA count is more likely overhead than occupancy gain.

Selected policy: **E16-only M-grid split from v160**.

**SELECTED_ARCHITECTURE**

Prototype v170 as `v160` plus E16-only elementwise M-grid split:

- Keep `_EXPERT_BLOCK_M = 192`.
- Keep tensor shapes, BMMs, chunks, workspace, and v160 E16 FC1 order/in-place behavior unchanged.
- Add `m_tiles = 3`, `block_m = 64`.
- Apply only when `num_experts == 16` by generating E16-specific pack, guarded SwiGLU, and unpack kernels.
- Leave E32/E64 exactly on v160 kernels.

**KERNEL_GRID_CHANGES**

For E16 pack:

```python
with T.Kernel(num_experts, 3, T.ceildiv(hidden, block_n), threads=threads) as (expert_id, bm, bn):
    group_size = group_sizes[expert_id]
    padded_start = group_padded_offsets[expert_id]
    for i, j in T.Parallel(64, block_n):
        if bm * 64 + i < group_size:
            packed_input[expert_id, bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                stacked_expert_tokens[padded_start + bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j]
            )
```

For E16 SwiGLU:

```python
with T.Kernel(num_experts, 3, T.ceildiv(intermediate, block_n), threads=threads) as (expert_id, bm, bn):
    group_size = group_sizes[expert_id]
    for i, j in T.Parallel(64, block_n):
        if bm * 64 + i < group_size:
            gate = gate_output[expert_id, bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j]
            activation[expert_id, bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                gate * T.sigmoid(gate) * up_output[expert_id, bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j]
            )
```

For E16 unpack:

```python
with T.Kernel(expert_chunk, 3, T.ceildiv(hidden, block_n), threads=threads) as (local_expert_id, bm, bn):
    expert_id = expert_start + local_expert_id
    group_size = group_sizes[expert_id]
    raw_start = group_offsets[expert_id]
    padded_start = group_padded_offsets[expert_id]
    for i, j in T.Parallel(64, block_n):
        if bm * 64 + i < group_size:
            out[padded_start + bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                packed_output[local_expert_id, bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j]
                * routed_expert_weights[raw_start + bm * 64 + i]
            )
```

**INDEXING_PROOF**

`bm in [0,2]`, `i in [0,63]`, so `bm * 64 + i in [0,191]`, exactly covering old `i in [0,191]`.

The guard `bm * 64 + i < group_size` preserves all valid-row behavior and still skips padding rows. `bn * block_n + j` is unchanged from v160; hidden/intermediate divisibility is unchanged and current kernels already rely on the same `block_n=256` extent.

No row is missed and no row is duplicated because each row has exactly one decomposition:

```text
bm = row // 64
i  = row % 64
```

**RACE_PROOF**

Avoid local scalar row assignments inside `T.Parallel`. Use direct expressions only:

- Good: `bm * 64 + i`
- Avoid: `row = bm * 64 + i` inside `T.Parallel`

Each CTA writes a unique `(expert, row, col)` coordinate. Different `bm` values have disjoint row intervals:

```text
bm=0 -> rows 0..63
bm=1 -> rows 64..127
bm=2 -> rows 128..191
```

Unpack writes unique `out[padded_start + row, col]` for the same reason. No inter-CTA overlap is introduced.

**WORK_AND_OCCUPANCY_MODEL**

E16 old elementwise CTA counts:

```text
pack:   16 * ceil(2048/256) = 128
SwiGLU: 16 * ceil(8192/256) = 512
unpack: 16 * ceil(2048/256) = 128
total:  768 CTAs
```

E16 new:

```text
pack:   384
SwiGLU: 1536
unpack: 384
total:  2304 CTAs
```

Total element count is unchanged. Per-CTA work drops from `192 * 256` logical elements to `64 * 256`, while CTA scheduling count rises 3x.

This is favorable only if the old 192-row CTA had poor per-CTA latency/occupancy or long serialized per-thread work. It is least attractive for E64 because E64 already has 4096 elementwise CTAs before splitting.

**EXPECTED_GAIN**

Expected whole-shape gain for E16: **0.2% to 0.8%**, roughly `0.004-0.016 ms` against v160’s `2.018 ms`.

A larger gain is unlikely because BMMs dominate. Regressions are plausible if CTA scheduling overhead exceeds latency hiding.

Expected E32/E64 if all-shape were used: likely neutral to negative, especially E64.

**RISKS**

- TileLang may generate more overhead for the extra grid axis than expected.
- 3x CTAs may hurt launch-side scheduling or SM block dispatch.
- E16 gain may be below judge noise.
- If `T.Parallel(64,256)` maps less efficiently than `T.Parallel(192,256)`, the change can regress despite lower work per CTA.
- Need C500 compile/correctness once access recovers; v169 access issue means do not submit blindly.

**IMPLEMENTATION_INSTRUCTIONS**

Create v170 from `v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py`.

Implement separate E16-only kernels, or parameterize with `m_grid_split` in cache key. Prefer separate kernels to keep E32/E64 byte-for-byte behavior close to v160.

Required edits:

- Add E16 pack kernel with grid `(num_experts, 3, ceildiv(hidden, 256))`.
- Add E16 SwiGLU kernel with grid `(num_experts, 3, ceildiv(intermediate, 256))`.
- Add E16 unpack kernel with grid `(expert_chunk, 3, ceildiv(hidden, 256))`.
- Use `T.Parallel(64, 256)`.
- Use direct `bm * 64 + i` expressions in guards and indices.
- Do not change BMM order, FC2 chunking, workspace shapes, `_EXPERT_BLOCK_M`, math, or E32/E64 paths.
- Validate all three shapes when C500 access returns; compare only fresh XPUOJ timings after Accepted.

**FINAL_GATE**

`PASS_TO_IMPLEMENT`

Implement **E16-only M-grid split** as v170. Avoid all-shape for the first prototype; it mixes a plausible E16 occupancy win with likely E32/E64 CTA overhead.
tokens used
300,966
**DUPLICATE_AUDIT**

No duplicate found for the proposed pack/SwiGLU/unpack M-grid split. Prior related work was different:

- v131/v139: elementwise `block_n=512`, width-only, both regressed.
- v123-v125: `block_n=128/512`-style elementwise width probes, not an M-axis grid split.
- v140: `block_m=64` inside a custom fused FC1/SwiGLU TileLang GEMM path, not current pack/SwiGLU/unpack kernels.
- v163: M split inside custom FC2 tail, retired because backend BMM dominated.

So this is a materially new elementwise CTA-grid architecture.

**SHAPE_POLICY**

Ranking:

1. `E16-only`: best risk/reward. E16 has the lowest existing elementwise CTA count and v160’s only positive signal.
2. `all-shape`: broader experiment, but likely lets E32/E64 scheduling overhead erase E16 gains.
3. `E64-only`: weakest. E64 already has enough elementwise CTAs; 3x CTA count is more likely overhead than occupancy gain.

Selected policy: **E16-only M-grid split from v160**.

**SELECTED_ARCHITECTURE**

Prototype v170 as `v160` plus E16-only elementwise M-grid split:

- Keep `_EXPERT_BLOCK_M = 192`.
- Keep tensor shapes, BMMs, chunks, workspace, and v160 E16 FC1 order/in-place behavior unchanged.
- Add `m_tiles = 3`, `block_m = 64`.
- Apply only when `num_experts == 16` by generating E16-specific pack, guarded SwiGLU, and unpack kernels.
- Leave E32/E64 exactly on v160 kernels.

**KERNEL_GRID_CHANGES**

For E16 pack:

```python
with T.Kernel(num_experts, 3, T.ceildiv(hidden, block_n), threads=threads) as (expert_id, bm, bn):
    group_size = group_sizes[expert_id]
    padded_start = group_padded_offsets[expert_id]
    for i, j in T.Parallel(64, block_n):
        if bm * 64 + i < group_size:
            packed_input[expert_id, bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                stacked_expert_tokens[padded_start + bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j]
            )
```

For E16 SwiGLU:

```python
with T.Kernel(num_experts, 3, T.ceildiv(intermediate, block_n), threads=threads) as (expert_id, bm, bn):
    group_size = group_sizes[expert_id]
    for i, j in T.Parallel(64, block_n):
        if bm * 64 + i < group_size:
            gate = gate_output[expert_id, bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j]
            activation[expert_id, bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                gate * T.sigmoid(gate) * up_output[expert_id, bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j]
            )
```

For E16 unpack:

```python
with T.Kernel(expert_chunk, 3, T.ceildiv(hidden, block_n), threads=threads) as (local_expert_id, bm, bn):
    expert_id = expert_start + local_expert_id
    group_size = group_sizes[expert_id]
    raw_start = group_offsets[expert_id]
    padded_start = group_padded_offsets[expert_id]
    for i, j in T.Parallel(64, block_n):
        if bm * 64 + i < group_size:
            out[padded_start + bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j] = (
                packed_output[local_expert_id, bm * 64 + i, bn * block_n + j]
                * routed_expert_weights[raw_start + bm * 64 + i]
            )
```

**INDEXING_PROOF**

`bm in [0,2]`, `i in [0,63]`, so `bm * 64 + i in [0,191]`, exactly covering old `i in [0,191]`.

The guard `bm * 64 + i < group_size` preserves all valid-row behavior and still skips padding rows. `bn * block_n + j` is unchanged from v160; hidden/intermediate divisibility is unchanged and current kernels already rely on the same `block_n=256` extent.

No row is missed and no row is duplicated because each row has exactly one decomposition:

```text
bm = row // 64
i  = row % 64
```

**RACE_PROOF**

Avoid local scalar row assignments inside `T.Parallel`. Use direct expressions only:

- Good: `bm * 64 + i`
- Avoid: `row = bm * 64 + i` inside `T.Parallel`

Each CTA writes a unique `(expert, row, col)` coordinate. Different `bm` values have disjoint row intervals:

```text
bm=0 -> rows 0..63
bm=1 -> rows 64..127
bm=2 -> rows 128..191
```

Unpack writes unique `out[padded_start + row, col]` for the same reason. No inter-CTA overlap is introduced.

**WORK_AND_OCCUPANCY_MODEL**

E16 old elementwise CTA counts:

```text
pack:   16 * ceil(2048/256) = 128
SwiGLU: 16 * ceil(8192/256) = 512
unpack: 16 * ceil(2048/256) = 128
total:  768 CTAs
```

E16 new:

```text
pack:   384
SwiGLU: 1536
unpack: 384
total:  2304 CTAs
```

Total element count is unchanged. Per-CTA work drops from `192 * 256` logical elements to `64 * 256`, while CTA scheduling count rises 3x.

This is favorable only if the old 192-row CTA had poor per-CTA latency/occupancy or long serialized per-thread work. It is least attractive for E64 because E64 already has 4096 elementwise CTAs before splitting.

**EXPECTED_GAIN**

Expected whole-shape gain for E16: **0.2% to 0.8%**, roughly `0.004-0.016 ms` against v160’s `2.018 ms`.

A larger gain is unlikely because BMMs dominate. Regressions are plausible if CTA scheduling overhead exceeds latency hiding.

Expected E32/E64 if all-shape were used: likely neutral to negative, especially E64.

**RISKS**

- TileLang may generate more overhead for the extra grid axis than expected.
- 3x CTAs may hurt launch-side scheduling or SM block dispatch.
- E16 gain may be below judge noise.
- If `T.Parallel(64,256)` maps less efficiently than `T.Parallel(192,256)`, the change can regress despite lower work per CTA.
- Need C500 compile/correctness once access recovers; v169 access issue means do not submit blindly.

**IMPLEMENTATION_INSTRUCTIONS**

Create v170 from `v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py`.

Implement separate E16-only kernels, or parameterize with `m_grid_split` in cache key. Prefer separate kernels to keep E32/E64 byte-for-byte behavior close to v160.

Required edits:

- Add E16 pack kernel with grid `(num_experts, 3, ceildiv(hidden, 256))`.
- Add E16 SwiGLU kernel with grid `(num_experts, 3, ceildiv(intermediate, 256))`.
- Add E16 unpack kernel with grid `(expert_chunk, 3, ceildiv(hidden, 256))`.
- Use `T.Parallel(64, 256)`.
- Use direct `bm * 64 + i` expressions in guards and indices.
- Do not change BMM order, FC2 chunking, workspace shapes, `_EXPERT_BLOCK_M`, math, or E32/E64 paths.
- Validate all three shapes when C500 access returns; compare only fresh XPUOJ timings after Accepted.

**FINAL_GATE**

`PASS_TO_IMPLEMENT`

Implement **E16-only M-grid split** as v170. Avoid all-shape for the first prototype; it mixes a plausible E16 occupancy win with likely E32/E64 CTA overhead.
